# capability-manifest.md — rusty_goo_adk (Rust port of google/adk-python)

This is the running source of truth for the migration, per the `rust-migration`
skill's boundary contract: **every row defaults to REQUIRED.** A row only
moves to `OUT-OF-SCOPE` with a written, user-attributed reason; a row only
moves to `DONE` with a linked PR and named parity test. Nothing is dropped by
omission. `scripts/check_manifest_coverage.sh` fails loudly if any row is
neither `DONE` nor `OUT-OF-SCOPE`.

Source repo inventoried: [google/adk-python](https://github.com/google/adk-python)
at the commit cloned 2026-08-21 (~206k lines, 28 top-level modules under
`src/google/adk/`). Inventory performed by 8 parallel read-only agents per
`src/google/adk/{module}/` groupings; full per-module notes are preserved in
this repo's development history (see the rust-migration skill's scratch
output for this run) and can be pulled back up if a row's evidence needs
expansion before it's worked.

## Phases

Capabilities are grouped into phases in rough dependency order — a phase is a
sequencing device for the roadmap, not a scope cut. All phases are in scope;
later phases just depend on earlier ones landing first (e.g. you need a
session backend before compaction has anything to compact). Work phases in
order; within a phase, order is up to whoever's picking up the next issue.

**Note on format**: this list is deliberately plain text, not a markdown
table — `check_manifest_coverage.sh` assumes exactly one pipe-table in the
whole file (the capability table far below) and locates its columns from
the *first* header row it sees; a second table here would be misread as
that first header and break the coverage check.

- **P1 — Core runtime primitives**: `platform/` (random/time/uuid/thread providers), `errors/`, `events/`
- **P2 — Agent core & context**: `agents/` base classes (`BaseAgent`, `Context`/`InvocationContext`/`ReadonlyContext`, `RunConfig`, `LiveRequestQueue`), `LlmAgent` core
- **P3 — Model layer core**: `models/` base (`BaseLlm`, `LlmRequest`/`LlmResponse`, `LLMRegistry`, `CacheMetadata`), native Gemini backend
- **P4 — LLM flow engine**: `flows/llm_flows/` (`BaseLlmFlow`, request/response processors, `functions.py`, audio/transcription), `planners/`
- **P5 — Sessions**: `sessions/` — all 4 backends, schemas, migration tooling
- **P6 — Memory & artifacts**: `memory/` (3 backends), `artifacts/` (3 backends)
- **P7 — Multi-agent & workflow**: `apps/` (incl. compaction), `workflow/` (graph engine), `agents/` subclasses (Sequential/Parallel/Loop/RemoteA2a/Managed/LangGraph), `plugins/`
- **P8 — Tools core & built-ins**: `tools/` core infra + standalone built-in tools, `skills/`, `code_executors/`
- **P9 — Auth & credentials**: `auth/` — full credential lifecycle
- **P10 — Additional model providers & labs**: `models/` (Anthropic, LiteLLM, Apigee, Gemma, Interactions API), `labs/` (OpenAI, Antigravity)
- **P11 — Evaluation & optimization**: `evaluation/`, `optimization/`
- **P12 — Telemetry & feature flags**: `telemetry/`, `features/`, `dependencies/`
- **P13 — CLI & servers**: `cli/` — commands, HTTP/RPC server (dev + prod), deploy pipelines
- **P14 — A2A protocol**: `a2a/`
- **P15 — Cloud integrations**: `integrations/` — all cloud-service integrations, Google API toolsets
- **P16 — Repo-wide / build & packaging**: pyproject.toml-equivalent (Cargo features), CI, docs-as-spec items
- **P17 — Deferred / standalone decisions**: bundled web UI (8.3MB Angular bundle), `examples/`

## RustyMill sibling-check methodology (step 1 pass)

A full `scan_platform_repos.sh` run per capability (~1000 rows) wasn't done in
this pass — infeasible at this granularity in one sitting. The **Existing
RustyMill impl** column below is populated only where a repo/purpose match is
obvious from `references/platform-directory.md`'s name/purpose heuristic
(confirm-don't-assume, per that file's own caveat); most rows are blank.
Step 3 re-runs `scan_platform_repos.sh` for real before hand-rolling each
capability when its issue is actually worked, so a blank cell here is not a
final answer — just an unconfirmed one. Candidates worth checking first based
on purpose overlap: `rusty_json` (serialization), `rusty_http`/`rusty_request`
(HTTP client), `rusty_url` (URL parsing), `rusty_tls` (TLS/mTLS), `rusty_db`
(session/DB backends), `rusty_provider` (LLM provider abstraction —
directly relevant to `models/`), `rusty_llama` (local LLM inference),
`rusty_search` (retrieval/search abstraction), `rusty_compactor` (token
compaction — directly relevant to `apps/compaction.py`), `rusty_wire` (binary
serialization), `rusty_tokio` (async runtime), `rusty_prime_agent` (Rust
rewrite of another agent daemon's session/worker core — check for reusable
runtime-loop patterns).

## Columns

Column order in the single table below: **ID, Phase, Capability, Category,
Source, Existing RustyMill impl, Status, Reason (if OUT-OF-SCOPE), Evidence**
— same semantics as `references/capability-manifest-format.md`, with `Phase`
inserted per the roadmap-by-phases request. `Category` is `interface` /
`config` / `behavior` per `SKILL.md` step 1. `Source` is how the row was
found: `interface` (public API/CLI/route surface), `code` (behavior read
directly from implementation), `test` (a passing source-repo test encodes
it), `docs` (README/docs/CHANGELOG) — often combined (e.g. `code+test`).

There is exactly **one** markdown table in this file, immediately below —
`check_manifest_coverage.sh` locates columns by header name and assumes a
single continuous table; a second header row would be mis-parsed as data.

Still genuinely `Partial:` and NOT wired into `LlmFlow::preprocess`: the source auto-builds `tools_dict` from `LlmAgent.canonical_tools()` (plus a synthesized transfer tool), which needs the `BaseAgent`/`LlmAgent` tree fusion (C0092, still standing-blocked, the same row `agent_transfer.rs` itself is blocked on) — wiring this into `preprocess` today would only ever see an empty `tools_dict`, turning every confirmation attempt into a "tool is not registered" error instead of the source's real behavior, so it's left unwired and ready for a future C0092-unblocking batch. |

Ported: `_as_function_response_part`/`_extract_media_from_entry`/`_extract_multimodal_parts` all in full, including the exact depth-bounded recursion (`_MAX_MEDIA_CONTAINER_DEPTH = 1`: one level of container nesting is inspected, a container found at that depth is kept as-is rather than recursed into a second time) and the "`remaining or {}`" empty-container coercion. This is looser than the source's identity check (a plain dict that happens to carry `inlineData`/`fileData`-shaped keys would also match here), but it's the only representation a tool constructing media in this port actually has to work with. | C0336 | P7 | `ParallelAgent` (deprecated → Workflow) — concurrent sub-agent execution in isolated sub-branches, merges event streams (TaskGroup on 3.11+, manual management below); `escalate` cancels remaining siblings early | interface/behavior | code+test | — | REQUIRED | | Partial: crates/adk-agents/src/parallel_agent.rs::{ParallelAgent, branch_ctx_for_sub_agent, tests::runs_every_sub_agent_concurrently_and_collects_all_events, tests::returns_no_events_without_sub_agents, tests::merges_every_sub_agents_state_delta_onto_the_parent_context, tests::each_sub_agent_runs_in_its_own_branch, tests::resumable_run_emits_start_and_end_of_agent_markers, tests::skips_a_sub_agent_already_marked_finished_in_a_previous_run, tests::run_live_impl_is_not_supported}. Genuine concurrent sub-agent execution (`rusty_tokio::spawn`, one task per sub-agent), each in its own isolated branch (`BranchPath::create_sub_branch`, C0031, already built). Adaptation, disclosed at length in the module doc: this port's `AgentBehavior` returns a fully-collected `Vec<Event>` (not a live stream, already disclosed in `base_agent.rs`), so there's no partial result to race/cancel mid-flight — sub-agents run with real concurrency, but `escalate`-triggered early cancellation of still-running siblings isn't implemented (a sibling already in flight when one escalates finishes normally). This port's `InvocationContext::clone()` is a real deep clone (unlike the source's shallow `model_copy()`, whose nested dicts stay shared across every branch), so each sub-agent's `state_delta` is applied onto the parent's `ctx.session.state` post-hoc to restore cross-branch visibility for whatever runs after; full nested-resumability propagation (a sub-agent's own internal `agent_states`/`end_of_agents` writes reaching the parent mid-turn) is narrower — "all sub-agents finished" is derived from this turn completing without a pause, not from replaying each sub-agent's own nested agent-state events. Not ported: `ParallelAgentConfig`/YAML config loading (C0338, needs the config-resolution pipeline C0348 discloses as unbuilt). `_run_live_impl` matches the source exactly — it raises `NotImplementedError` upstream too, never implemented there either. |

Fundamental adaptation, disclosed at length in the module doc: Rust has no runtime function-signature reflection, so `FunctionTool::new` takes an already-built `FunctionDeclaration` and an explicit `required_args` list rather than deriving either from the wrapped closure (no `build_function_declaration`/`lru_cache`/`_get_mandatory_args` equivalent); the wrapped closure always takes `(&BTreeMap<String, Value>, &mut ToolContext)` (no context-parameter auto-detection needed, since the signature is fixed rather than discovered); no `_preprocess_args` Pydantic coercion (the closure body converts its own args via `rusty_serde::json::from_value` as needed); no sync/async runner distinction (every closure is already async by construction). Disclosed narrowings (module doc, at length): `max_memory_bytes`/`max_file_size_bytes`/`max_child_processes`/`RLIMIT_CORE` resource limits aren't enforced (no `libc`/`setrlimit` binding, and adding one wasn't judged worth it for three `setrlimit` calls — so those fields don't exist on this port's `BashToolPolicy` rather than existing unenforced); a timeout kills only the immediate child (`Command::kill_on_drop`), not its whole process group (`Child` has no `killpg`-equivalent) — a grandchild can survive; a timeout's response carries no partial stdout/stderr (this port's `Child::wait_with_output` doesn't support the source's kill-then-redrain pattern); `shlex.split` is a hand-rolled POSIX-ish word splitter (quotes + backslash escaping), not the full shlex grammar — though since the source's own `create_subprocess_exec` never runs a shell either, shell operators are literal argv words in both ports regardless. |

| C0427 | P8 | `load_web_page` — fetches a URL, extracts text via BeautifulSoup+lxml; **substantial SSRF hardening**: rejects non-http(s)/localhost, resolves hostname and rejects non-global IPs incl. IPv4-embedded-in-IPv6 (mapped/6to4/NAT64/deprecated-compat forms), IP-pinned connection with original Host header (DNS-rebinding/TOCTOU defense), redirects disabled | interface/behavior/security | code | — | REQUIRED | | Partial: crates/adk-tools/src/load_web_page.rs::{load_web_page, LoadWebPageTool, ip_classification::*, tests::*}. The SSRF-hardening core is ported in full: URL scheme/host/port validation, `localhost`/`*.localhost` rejection, DNS resolution with every resolved address vetted before any connection, IP-literal vetting, IPv4/IPv6 global-reachability classification transcribed field-for-field from CPython 3.11's own `ipaddress.py` (consulted locally at `/usr/lib/python3.11/ipaddress.py` — the exact reference the source's `is_global` resolves to; ground-truthed against a 30+-address battery run through the real `ipaddress` module before writing the Rust tests), the embedded-IPv4-in-IPv6 checks (mapped/6to4/NAT64/deprecated-IPv4-compatible forms — verified this catches cases the raw IPv6 `is_global` check alone misses, e.g. `64:ff9b::169.254.169.254` and `::169.254.169.254` both read as "global" by the plain check but correctly blocked via the embedded-address check, matching the source's own reasoning), IP-pinned connection via `reqwest::blocking::ClientBuilder::resolve` (not a hand-rolled connection adapter — cleaner than the source's own `_PinnedAddressAdapter`, since `resolve()` keeps the request URL pointed at the original hostname so the correct Host header is sent automatically), and disabled redirects. New `adk-tools` dependencies: `reqwest` (already a workspace dependency via `adk-models`/`gemini.rs`, new usage site only), `url` (the exact crate `reqwest::Url` re-exports, added explicitly for `url::Host`'s already-parsed Domain/Ipv4/Ipv6 variant), `regex` (already a workspace dependency, new usage site). Disclosed narrowings (module doc, at length): no proxy-aware branching (never reads `HTTP_PROXY`/`HTTPS_PROXY`/`NO_PROXY`, always does the direct IP-pinned fetch — strictly more restrictive, not a safety regression, but a real behavior gap for proxy-dependent environments); HTML text extraction has no HTML5 parser behind it (a regex-based tag/script/style stripper plus a handful of common named-entity decodes, not `BeautifulSoup`+`lxml`); the live-fetch path itself has no automated test in this sandboxed environment (network/DNS-dependent, and the tool's own design correctly rejects the only reachable test targets like `127.0.0.1`/`localhost` before any request is attempted) — every other piece (URL parsing, hostname blocking, the full IP-classification battery, HTML extraction) is covered by 15 new tests. |

The source's abstract class overrides only `_get_declaration`; ported as a free function `retrieval_tool_declaration(name, description)` (same shape as `request_input_tool.rs`'s own `parameters_schema()` helper) plus a thin marker supertrait `BaseRetrievalTool: BaseTool` whose default `retrieval_declaration()` method wraps it — Rust can't have a subtrait override an inherited default trait method for just its own implementers, so a future concrete retrieval tool's own `get_declaration()` calls `self.retrieval_declaration()` explicitly. Gated on `FeatureName::JsonSchemaForFuncDecl` exactly as the source is (`parameters_json_schema` when on, typed `parameters` when off — both already opaque `Value` fields on this port's `FunctionDeclaration`, so both branches build the identical JSON-Schema-shaped literal). `run_async`'s abstract "expects `args[\"query\"]`" contract is documented, not implemented, since `BaseTool::run_async`'s existing default already errors for exactly this must-override case. The marker trait also gives a future port of `cli.agent_graph`'s `isinstance(tool_or_agent, BaseRetrievalTool)` checks (C0281, docs-only, deferred) something real to check against once that lands — built ahead of that still-blocked caller. Concrete subclasses `FilesRetrieval` (C0483), `LlamaIndexRetrieval` (C0484), `VertexAiRagRetrieval` (C0485) stay their own REQUIRED rows, each blocked on its own not-yet-adopted dependency (LlamaIndex, Vertex AI RAG). |

Disclosed adaptation: this port's `ArtifactService` trait is fully synchronous and `&self`-only, so unlike the source (which awaits the parent's own async `save_artifact` method inline, updating its `artifact_delta` action synchronously mid-run) this port accumulates saved versions in a shared map and merges them into the parent tool context's actions once the nested run completes — the same post-hoc-merge idiom `agent_tool.rs` already uses for state deltas. Reads and the write itself still route straight through to the parent's real backend as they happen; only the delta bookkeeping is deferred. Not ported: `_automatic_function_calling_util.py`, `_function_parameter_parse_util.py`, `_function_tool_declarations.py`, `_gda_stream_util.py`. |

| C0590 | P11 | Metric `response_match_score`(`RougeEvaluator`) — ROUGE-1 F-measure with a **custom Unicode-aware tokenizer** correctly handling CJK/Thai/Lao/Khmer/Myanmar scripts the stock ASCII tokenizer would score as zero | interface/behavior | code | — | DONE | | crates/adk-eval/src/final_response_match_v1.rs::{RougeEvaluator, RougeEvaluator::new, tests::*} (10 tests), crates/adk-eval/src/rouge.rs::{tokenize, score, tests::*} (10 tests incl. `matches_rouge_score_end_to_end`), crates/adk-eval/src/porter_stemmer.rs::{stem, tests::*} (5 tests incl. `matches_nltk_across_the_fixture`, 409 word/stem pairs). Hand-ported, not stubbed: (1) `nltk.stem.porter.PorterStemmer` (`NLTK_EXTENSIONS` mode — the only mode `rouge_score`'s `DefaultTokenizer` ever constructs; `MARTIN_EXTENSIONS`/`ORIGINAL_ALGORITHM` branches omitted as unreached), verified against 409 `(word, stem)` pairs actually generated by running real `nltk` 3.10.3 in this environment, not reconstructed from memory alone — an earlier hand-derived expectation table (from reading Porter's paper's own per-step examples in isolation) was caught wrong by this cross-check (e.g. `"agreed"` chains through step 1b to `"agree"` then step 5a strips the trailing `e` to `"agre"`, which a single-step reading misses) and replaced with the verified fixture. (2) `rouge_score`'s `_UnicodeAwareTokenizer` (CJK single-character splitting, Thai/Lao/Khmer/Myanmar grapheme-cluster grouping via combining-mark detection, ASCII words routed through the stemmer) and `RougeScorer`'s `rouge1` unigram-overlap scoring (`rougeL`/`rougeLsum`/`rouge2+`/`score_multi` unreached by this consumer, not ported). End-to-end cross-checked against the real upstream `rouge_score` source (fetched from `google-research/google-research` and run locally under real `nltk`) over 11 candidate/reference pairs spanning plain ASCII, stemming-sensitive, CJK, Thai, and punctuation-heavy text — all match to floating-point precision. **New dependency**: `unicode-general-category` (zero transitive dependencies, `no_std`, static UCD 16.0.0 tables) for Unicode General-Category (Mark) classification, needed for the source's `unicodedata.category(char).startswith("M")` checks across arbitrary scripts (Thai/Lao/Khmer/Myanmar combining marks plus e.g. Devanagari matras) — hand-embedding an accurate multi-script Mark-category table was judged impractical vs. this narrow, well-audited, non-sovereignty-sensitive crate (same bar as the earlier `regex` adoption). **Disclosed narrowing**: `unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", text)` is skipped (no normalization-table dependency added) — affects only compatibility-decomposable characters (full-width variants, some ligatures), not the common case; see the `rouge` module doc. |

Disclosed, at length in the module doc: this port's `feature_config` (C0643-C0646) is already a fixed, exhaustive `match` (a prior batch's own design choice, not new here) — every `FeatureName` carries exactly one hardcoded stage baked in at one place, so there's no way for a caller to independently assert a *different* stage the way choosing the wrong Python decorator could. That makes the source's three-way `working_in_progress`/`experimental`/`stable` split, and its stage-mismatch check, structurally moot rather than narrowed — collapsed into the one `check_feature_enabled` guard, which is the actual shared runtime behavior all three perform (raise unless enabled at call time). Disclosed narrowing: Rust has no `Exception`/`BaseException`/`SystemExit`/`CancelledError` hierarchy to distinguish — the structural analog is `join()`'s own `Result`; a *panic* on the background thread (this port's only abnormal-termination case, since there's no cancellation/`SystemExit` concept to preserve) is caught by `join()`'s `Err` case and re-wrapped into `RunnerError::AgentRun` naming the panic payload, rather than re-panicking the calling thread. |

| C0939 | — | `_lazy.accessors` — builds a package's `__getattr__`/`__dir__` for PEP 562 lazy submodule re-exports (resolves a heavyweight submodule only on first attribute access, caching it on the package thereafter) — **flagged as likely inapplicable to a Rust port, not silently ported or marked out-of-scope**: this is machinery for Python's specific dynamic-module-attribute-resolution protocol (`__getattr__` on a package, `importlib.import_module`, dunder-probe suppression for pickling/copying). Rust modules resolve entirely at compile time; there is no runtime module-attribute hook, no lazy-re-export mechanism, and no analog to `importlib`. This capability's *purpose* (avoid eagerly pulling in a heavyweight optional submodule) may not even apply the same way to a compiled, statically-linked Rust workspace — but per the boundary contract that's the user's call, not Claude's to decide unilaterally, so this stays `REQUIRED` rather than assumed out-of-scope | interface/behavior | code | — | REQUIRED | | |

| C0940 | — | `context_utils.Aclosing`/`_is_context_type`/`find_context_parameter` — `Aclosing` re-exports `contextlib.aclosing` for backward compatibility; `find_context_parameter` uses `inspect.signature`/`typing.get_type_hints` reflection to locate which parameter of an arbitrary callable is annotated `Context`/`ToolContext`/`CallbackContext`, so a caller (`function_tool.py`, `mcp_tool.py`, the automatic-function-calling util, the Eventarc/workflow integrations) knows whether and where to inject one | interface/behavior | code | — | REQUIRED | | **Flagged as inapplicable via a different, already-adopted mechanism, not silently ported or marked out-of-scope**: `Aclosing` is a re-export of an async-generator-closing context manager with no Rust equivalent (this port has no async-generator protocol to close). `find_context_parameter`'s underlying *purpose* — deciding whether/where a wrapped callable wants a `Context` — is already handled differently and disclosed at length in `adk-tools::function_tool.rs`'s own module doc (C0404): since Rust functions have no runtime-inspectable signatures, this port's `FunctionTool` always calls its wrapped closure with a fixed `(&BTreeMap<String, Value>, &mut ToolContext)` signature rather than discovering whether the closure wants one. That resolves this file's role for the one caller this workspace has actually built (`FunctionTool`); `mcp_tool.py`/the automatic-function-calling util/the Eventarc and workflow integrations aren't ported here, so whether their own uses of `find_context_parameter` need a comparable compile-time-signature substitute is unresolved — left `REQUIRED` rather than assumed closed by C0404's precedent alone. |
| C0941 | — | `agent_info.get_tools_info`/`get_agents_dict`/`AgentInfo` — recursively builds an introspectable info dict for an `LlmAgent` and its sub-agent tree (name/description/instruction/tool declarations/sub-agent names), resolving `BaseToolset`s and wrapping bare callables in `FunctionTool` along the way | interface/behavior | code | — | REQUIRED | | **Not yet ported — blocked on prerequisite architecture, not merely "larger than this batch"** (corrected after investigating for a follow-up batch): `LlmAgent` (`crates/adk-agents/src/llm_agent.rs`) has no `name`/`description`/`sub_agents` fields at all — those live only on `BaseAgent`, and `LlmAgent` isn't yet wired into `BaseAgent`'s tree as an `AgentBehavior` impl (its own module doc discloses this, deferred to Phase 3/4/8). `LlmAgent::tools` is `Vec<ToolUnion>` where every variant is an opaque `Value` placeholder — "nothing resolves or calls a tool yet" per its own doc; the real resolution (C0092, `canonical_tools`) is itself still `REQUIRED`. So `get_tools_info`'s `isinstance(tool, BaseTool)`/`isinstance(tool, BaseToolset)`/bare-callable branches have no real `Arc<dyn BaseTool>`/`Arc<dyn BaseToolset>` handles to dispatch on yet, and `get_agents_dict`'s recursion has no `sub_agents` to walk. `BaseTool::get_declaration`/`BaseToolset::get_tools` (the per-tool pieces) are real and ready (`crates/adk-tools/src/{base_tool,base_toolset}.rs`); a `Tool { function_declarations }` wrapper struct doesn't exist yet either (`adk-genai::content` has only bare `FunctionDeclaration`). This capability can't land for real until C0092 and the `LlmAgent`-into-`BaseAgent` integration land first — left `REQUIRED`, not a small deferred batch. |

| C0943 | — | `yaml_utils.load_yaml_file`/`dump_pydantic_to_yaml` — loads a YAML file (`yaml.safe_load`); dumps a Pydantic model to YAML with `\|`-style multiline strings, sorted keys, unicode-unescaped, wrapping disabled | interface/behavior | code | — | REQUIRED | | **Flagged — needs a new dependency decision, not silently deferred or hand-rolled**: no YAML crate is a dependency anywhere in this workspace. Hand-rolling a YAML parser/dumper (unlike the small, well-defined algorithms this port has hand-rolled elsewhere, e.g. `civil_from_days`) would mean reimplementing a large, genuinely hairy spec (block/flow styles, anchors, multi-document streams, the custom `\|`-block multiline-string representer this file itself layers on top) — not "simple and deterministic" the way this port's hand-roll precedent requires. Left `REQUIRED`, flagged for the user to pick a YAML crate (or explicitly accept a narrower hand-rolled subset) before this can be built. |
| C0944 | — | `_schema_utils._strip_json_code_fence` — removes a markdown code fence wrapping an entire JSON payload, if present (a model occasionally wraps structured output in `` ```json ... ``` ``) | behavior | code | — | DONE | | crates/adk-genai/src/schema_utils.rs::{strip_json_code_fence, tests::strips_a_fence_with_a_language_tag, tests::strips_a_bare_fence_with_no_language_tag, tests::leaves_well_formed_unfenced_json_unchanged, tests::leaves_a_partial_or_malformed_fence_unchanged, tests::preserves_a_fence_nested_inside_the_payload}. Adaptation, disclosed in the module doc: the source's `re.fullmatch(..., re.DOTALL)` is hand-rolled as direct string slicing rather than adding `regex` as a new usage site of this crate — the fullmatch/DOTALL combination means the opening/closing `` ``` `` must be the string's literal first/last 3 characters, with no backtracking ambiguity a regex engine is actually needed to resolve. Python's Unicode-aware `\w` is approximated with `char::is_alphanumeric() \|\| c == '_'` rather than restricted to ASCII — closer to the source, not proven byte-identical across every Unicode category `\w` covers. |
| C0945 | — | `_schema_utils.is_basemodel_schema`/`is_list_of_basemodel`/`get_list_inner_type`/`schema_to_json_schema`/`validate_schema`/`validate_node_data` — generic runtime schema introspection and data validation/coercion against an arbitrary `SchemaType` (a `BaseModel` class, `list[SomeModel]`, a raw JSON-Schema `dict`, or `google.genai.types.Schema`), via `pydantic.TypeAdapter`/`get_origin`/`get_args` | interface/behavior | code | — | REQUIRED | | **Flagged — blocked on missing generic runtime type-reflection/validation machinery**, not merely unstarted: every one of these functions dispatches on an arbitrary Python *type object* at runtime (`isinstance(schema, type) and issubclass(schema, BaseModel)`, `get_origin`/`get_args` on a generic alias, `TypeAdapter(schema).validate_json(...)`/`.json_schema()`). Rust has no runtime type objects to introspect this way, and this workspace has no `TypeAdapter`-equivalent generic-validation layer — a caller would need to already know its concrete schema type at compile time, which is a different, caller-specific design each of this file's actual call sites would need individually, not a single generic port of this file. `_strip_json_code_fence`, the one piece with no such dependency, was split out and ported separately as C0944. |

Attribution: discovered 2026-08-24, added as C0948/C0949 in the same PR that closes them out (no separate manifest-only PR this time, since the whole gap could be closed in one batch). crates/adk-tools/src/base_environment.rs::{ExecutionResult, EnvironmentError, BaseEnvironment}. Adaptation, disclosed at length in the module doc: `is_initialized` (a property every subclass inherits for free from the source's class-level `_is_initialized` attribute) becomes a required trait method instead, since Rust traits carry no data — a concrete implementor backs it with its own interior-mutable state; `initialize()` returns `Result<(), EnvironmentError>` even in the trivial default case (the source lets a real initialize failure propagate as an uncaught exception, this port surfaces it via `Result` instead); `write_file(path, content: str \| bytes)` collapses to `&[u8]` without losing behavior (the source's str branch opens with `newline=''`, disabling translation, so both branches always produce byte-identical output); `path: Path` narrows to `&str` (every real caller in this batch only ever has a path as a string). |
| C0949 | P8 | `environment._local_environment` — `LocalEnvironment(BaseEnvironment)`: subprocess-shell `execute()` with timeout+kill, `read_file`/`write_file` via blocking I/O, path-escape rejection out of `working_dir`, auto-created-temp-dir lifecycle (created on `initialize()`, removed on `close()` only if auto-created) | interface/behavior/security | code | — | DONE | | crates/adk-tools/src/local_environment.rs::{LocalEnvironment, resolve_path, lexically_normalize, tests::*} (12 tests). Ported: shell-based `execute` (`sh -c <command>`) with timeout, exit-code/signal handling, stdout/stderr capture; `read_file`/`write_file` offloaded to `rusty_tokio::spawn_blocking`; auto-created-vs-explicit `working_dir` lifecycle (temp dir created+removed only when auto-created, matching the source's `_auto_created` flag exactly). Adaptations, disclosed at length in the module doc: interior mutability (`Mutex<Option<PathBuf>>`/`AtomicBool`s) replaces plain instance-attribute mutation, since `BaseEnvironment`'s methods take `&self` so `Arc<dyn BaseEnvironment>` can be shared across the toolset's four tools; `_resolve_path` is lexical (`os.path.normpath`-style `..`/`.` collapsing, no filesystem touch) rather than `Path.resolve()`-based, since a `write_file` target may not exist yet — same "path safety by construction, not by canonicalize" adaptation already established in `file_artifact_service.rs` (C0268-C0269), with the same disclosed symlink-following divergence. Disclosed narrowing (same one already established in `bash_tool.rs`, C0418): a timed-out command's `Child::wait_with_output` is consumed as one unit, so a timeout carries no partial stdout/stderr the way the source's re-invoked `communicate()` captures. |

Disclosed narrowing: the source's `ToolUnion`'s third member (a bare `Callable`, wrapped via `FunctionTool(callable)`'s `inspect.signature` reflection) has no port — `FunctionTool`'s own module doc (C0421/C0422's home) already discloses this port has no such runtime reflection, so `AdditionalTool` only models the two Rust-expressible branches (`Tool`/`Toolset`); this was a convenience overload in Python, not an independently-callable capability this port drops. `asyncio.gather(..., return_exceptions=True)` around toolset resolution has no Rust equivalent needed — `BaseToolset::get_tools_with_prefix` is already infallible in this port (C0403), so there's no exception path to catch. |
